NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
TUESDAY, July 10, 2012 
 
PRESENT: Jesse Thompson, Supervisor; Jerry Brownlow, Vice-Chairman; and J.R. DeSpain, Chairman were 
present. David Tenney, Supervisor and Jonathan M. Nez, Supervisor were not present.   
 
9:34 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation was led by Supervisor Thompson.  
 
CALL TO THE PUBLIC: There were no comments from the public.  
CONSENT AGENDA:  a) Minutes: April 10, 2012 and April 24, 2012  b) Justice Court Reports: Holbrook 
Precinct #1, June; Winslow Precinct #2, June; Snowflake Precinct #3, June; Kayenta  Precinct #4, June; Show 
Low Precinct #5, June; and Pinetop -Lakeside Precinct #6, June 2012  c) Clerk of Superior Court Report June 
2012 d) Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 June 2012  e) Tax Exemptions filed pursuant to A.R.S. §42 -
11153 as a Request for R edemption of Waiver for 45 properties  f) Conduct precinct -based election for 
Northland Pioneer College District on November 6, 2012 to elect 1 position for District #2 for the remainder of 
the appointed six -year term commencing on November 7, 2012 and expi ring December 31, 2016; to elect 1 
position for District #3, for the six-year term, commencing January 1, 2013 and expiring on December 31, 2019  
g) Conduct precinct-based election for Joseph City Domestic Water Improvement District on November 6, 2012 
to elect 2 positions with a 4 year term beginning January 1, 2013 and ending December 31, 2016  h) Conduct 
precinct-based election for Joseph City Sanitary District on November 6, 2012 to elect 2 positions with a 4 year 
term beginning January 1, 2013 and ending  December 31, 2016 i) Intergovernmental Agreement with the City 
of Show Low for continued participation in the Show Low Special Response Team  j) Arizona Criminal Justice 
Commission Byrne Grant #DC -13-001 for $245,193 for operation of the Major Crimes Appre hension Team k) 
Arizona Criminal Justice Commission Crime Victim Compensation Program Grant Agreement #VC -13-058 for 
$79,184.00 l) Arizona Criminal Justice Commission Drug, Gang, and Violent Crime Control Grant Agreement 
#DC-13-023 for $117,040.00  m) Letter of support for the Arizona Commerce Authority : Supervisor 
Brownlow made a motion  to authorize the Chairman to sign the items in the consent agenda as presented; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
a. Presentation to retiri ng employee Marjorie Brakefield : Chairman DeSpain made a presentation to 
Margie Brakefield for her years of service to Navajo County. Mrs. Brakefield has worked for the Navajo 
County from November of 1993 to July 20, 2012. Mrs. Brakefield indicated that she will be movi ng to 
Happy Jack and will also have a home in Tonto Basin. She will also be vacationing  and will miss 
everyone.  
b. Navajo County recognizes the teachers of the Junior Leadership Academy : Linda Morrow recognized 
Greg Shalow, Gil Alvidrez, Diana Butler, Beth Schimmel, Dr. Mary Jeanne Munroe, David Butterfield, 
and Debe Campbell for their dedication and leadership within this program . They were each presented 
with a certificate and burden basket form the Board of Su pervisors. Mrs. Morrow indicated that there 
were 80 students this year, working together and learning. Mrs. Morrow  spoke about the programs 
mission, projects, funding and outreach. M rs. Morrow also thanked the many partners that have helped 
sponsor this program. The individuals spoke of their experience with the Junior Leadership Academy. 
Supervisor Thompson thanked the group for their leadership. Supervisor Brownlow thanked the group 
for their volunteering for our community and for our children.  
c. Personnel Service Awards : David Coolidge recognized the following employees for their years of 
dedicated service to Navajo County: James Ronken, 5 years; Jason Twede, 5 years; William Sturgeon,

<!-- page 2 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 07-10-2012 
Page 2 of 5 
 
15 years; Sabrina James, 15 years; Brenda Clark, 20 years; Donna McMonigal, 20 years; Kelly Clark, 20 
years; and John Lamb, 20 years.  
HUMAN RESOURCES: Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions: David Coolidge reviewed 
the 12 item personnel action r eport. Supervisor Brownlow made a motion  to authorize the Chairman to sign 
the 12 item personnel action report; motion seconded by  Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving 
the motion.  
ASSESSOR: Consideration and possible approval of Settlement Agreement between Quality Hills and Navajo 
County regarding 2009 assessed value of 18 Navajo County parcels : Assessor Cammy Darris discussed the 
item. Mr. Carlyon indicated that given the cost, we believe that this settlement agreement is in the best interest 
of the County. Chairman DeSpain asked if the agreement would settle the litigation. Mr. Carlyon indicated that 
the settlement agreement would end any and all litigation regarding this item. Supervisor Thompson made a 
motion to approve the settlement agreement between Qua lity Hills and Navajo County regarding the 2009 
assessed value of 18 Navajo County parcels; motion seconded by  Supervisor Brownlow . Vote unanimous 
approving the motion.  
EMERGENCY MANAGEMENT: Report of current fire conditions and possible consideration and  approval 
of rescinding the Outdoor Fire Ordinance 01 -06, Section 7 for the 2012 fire season to coincide with the United 
States Forest Service and consultatio n with the local fire districts : Dan Hinz  discussed the item and gave an 
update on the forest serv ice restrictions. Mr. Hinz indicated that fire restrictions have been rescinded by the 
Forest Service as of 12:01 pm yesterday along with Pinetop -Lakeside, the City of Show Low and the Town of 
Snowflake. Mr. Hinz indicated that the fire restrict ions went into effect on May 15, 2012 of this year and that 
Emergency Management is now asking that the restrictions be lifted due to the moisture that we have  received. 
Supervisor Thompson made a motion  to rescind the Outdoor Fire Ordinance 01 -06, Section 7 for the 2012 
fire season to coincide with the United States Forest Service and in consultation with the local fire districts; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Brownlow. Vote unanimous approving the motion. Mary Springer highlighted 
the working group, whom has  been meeting regularly to share information. Dan recognized the support from 
Mary Springer, John Swanson, Montana Slack, Jeff Lineberry, Catrina Roe, Tim Norton and John Zimmerman. 
Mr. Jayne thanked the group and indicated that this team does more than anyone eve r sees. Supervisor 
Thompson thanked the group.  Supervisor Brownlow also thanked the group and indicated that we have 
learned a lot since the fires last year.  
PUBLIC WORKS: Consideration and possible award of Contract #B12 -04-002 Delivery and Application o f 
Magnesium Chloride and/or Magnesium Chloride/Lignin Blend to GMCO Corporation: Homero Vela discussed 
the item. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve the award of Contract #B12 -04-002 Delivery and 
Application of Magnesium Chloride and/or Magnesium Chloride/Lignin Blend to GMCO Corporation ; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Brownlow. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
FINANCE:  
a. Consideration and possible  adoption of the proposed Tentative Budget for Fiscal Year 2012 -2013 for 
Navajo County: James Menlove discussed the item .  Mr. Menlove discussed the handout titled Table of 
Contents Fiscal Year 2013 which is Schedules A -F (Resolution for the Adoption of th e Budget). There 
is a slight decrease in the budget from the previous year with the amount of $113,367,385.00. Mr. 
Menlove indicated that changes can still be made to the budget with the restrict ion that we cannot 
exceed the $113,367,385.00. Mr. Menlove ad ded that Navajo County continues to provide services to 
the citizens of Navajo County. Supervisor Brownlow asked about the secondary property taxes under

<!-- page 3 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 07-10-2012 
Page 3 of 5 
 
schedule B. Mr. Menlove indicated that we have recommended to keep the tax rate for secondary’s flat 
and explained that there has been an additional decrease in properties values, therefore homeowners will 
pay less in taxes in Navajo County. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to adopt the proposed 
Tentative Budget for Fiscal Year 2012 -2013 for Navajo County ; motion seconded by  Supervisor 
Brownlow. Vote unanimous approving the motion.

James Menlove expressed his appreciation to Cris Parisot, the Elected Officials and the Department Directors 
for their efforts in the budget process. Mr. Jayne expressed his gratitude for the Finance team, the Elected 
Officials and Department Directors.  
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Thompson reported that he will be attending the NACo Conference with Supervisor Tenney, 
Hunter Moore and Brad Carlyon. He further reported on the school bus routes and indicated that his aunt has 
passed away and he will be attending to the family needs . He also spoke of some spot graveling and noted that 
there was rain where his in-laws live. Supervisor Brownlow  reported on the Independence Day  events. He 
further reported on the rain in his district and fire season. Supervisor Brownlow thanked James and the Finance 
team for putting the budget together.  
ADJOURN: At 11:01 a.m. Supervisor Brownlow made a motion to adjourn; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.   
 
APPROVED: 
 
__________________________________ 
J.R. DeSpain, Chairman 
 
ATTEST: 
 
DATE: July 24, 2012 
 
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board
